Then she prayed toward the window, and said, Blessed art thou, O Lord my God, and thine holy and glorious name is blessed and honourable forever: let all thy works praise thee forever. And now, O Lord, I set mine eyes and my face toward thee, And say, Take me out of the earth, that I may hear no more the reproach. Thou knowest, Lord, that I am pure from all sin with man, And that I never polluted my name, nor the name of my father, in the land of my captivity: I am the only daughter of my father, neither hath he any child to be his heir, neither any near kinsman, nor any son of his alive, to whom I may keep myself for a wife: my seven husbands are already dead; and why should I live? but if it please not thee that I should die, command some regard to be had of me, and pity taken of me, that I hear no more reproach.

So the prayers of them both were heard before the majesty of the great God. And Raphael was sent to heal them both, that is, to scale away the whiteness of Tobit’s eyes, and to give Sara the daughter of Raguel for a wife to Tobias the son of Tobit; and to bind Asmodeus the evil spirit; because she belonged to Tobias by right of inheritance. The selfsame time came Tobit home, and entered into his house, and Sara the daughter of Raguel came down from her upper chamber.

===Chinese===

她听见这些话，心里非常忧愁，甚至想要自缢；她说：我是父亲独生的女儿，我若这样行，必成为他的羞辱，我要使他的白发悲悲惨惨地下阴间。于是她朝着窗户祷告，说：主我的神啊，你是应当称颂的，你圣洁荣耀的名配得称颂尊荣，直到永远；愿你一切的作为永远赞美你。主啊，如今我举目仰望你，说：求你使我离开大地，使我不再听见羞辱。主啊，你知道我洁净，未与男人犯罪；我没有污秽我的名，也没有污秽我父亲的名，在我被掳之地；我是父亲独生的女儿，他没有别的孩子作后嗣，也没有近亲，也没有在世的儿子，可以让我为他守身待嫁；我的七个丈夫都死了；我为什么还活着呢？你若不喜悦我死，求你吩咐人看顾我，怜恤我，使我不再听见羞辱。

他们二人的祷告，都在伟大的神威严之前蒙了垂听。拉斐耳被差去医治他们二人，就是除去托比眼睛的白翳，把拉贵尔的女儿撒拉给托比的儿子多比雅为妻，并捆绑恶灵阿斯摩太；因为她按继承之理原属于多比雅。正当那时，托比回到家里进了屋，拉贵尔的女儿撒拉也从她的楼上下来。

### IV / 四
===Original===

In that day Tobit remembered the money, which he had committed to Gabael in Rages of Media, And said with himself, I have wished for death; wherefore do I not call for my son Tobias, that I may signify to him of the money before I die?

===Chinese===

那一天，托比记起他曾托付给玛代拉吉斯加贝拉的银子，心里说：我既求死，为何不叫我的儿子多比雅来，好在死以前把银子的事告诉他呢？

===Original===

And when he had called him, he said, My son, when I am dead, bury me; and despise not thy mother, but honour her all the days of thy life, and do that which shall please her, and grieve her not. Remember, my son, that she saw many dangers for thee, when thou wast in her womb; and when she is dead, bury her by me in one grave. My son, be mindful of the Lord our God all thy days, and let not thy will be set to sin, or to transgress his commandments: do uprightly all thy life long, and follow not the ways of unrighteousness. For if thou deal truly, thy doings shall prosperously succeed to thee, and to all them that live justly. Give alms of thy substance; and when thou givest alms, let not thine eye be envious, neither turn thy face from any poor, and the face of God shall not be turned away from thee. If thou hast abundance, give alms accordingly: if thou have but a little, be not afraid to give according to that little: For thou layest up a good treasure for thyself against the day of necessity. Because that alms do deliver from death, and suffereth not to come into darkness. For alms is a good gift unto all that give it in the sight of the most High. Beware of all whoredom, my son, and chiefly take a wife of the seed of thy fathers, and take not a strange woman to wife, which is not of thy father’s tribe: for we are the children of the prophets, Noe, Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob: remember, my son, that our fathers from the beginning, even that they all married wives of their own kindred, and were blessed in their children, and their seed shall inherit the land. Now therefore, my son, love thy brethren, and despise not in thy heart thy brethren, the sons and daughters of thy people, in not taking a wife of them: for in pride is destruction and much trouble, and in lewdness is decay and great want: for lewdness is the mother of famine. Let not the wages of any man, which hath wrought for thee, tarry with thee, but give it to him out of hand: for if thou serve God, he will also repay thee: be circumspect, my son, in all things that thou doest, and be wise in all thy conversation. Do that to no man which thou hatest: drink not wine to make thee drunken: neither let drunkenness go with thee in thy journey.

===Chinese===

他叫了他来，说：我儿，我死了以后，你要葬埋我；不可轻看你的母亲，要终生尊敬她，行她喜悦的事，不可使她忧愁。我儿，要记念我们的主神，终生不可定意犯罪，也不可违背他的诫命；一生一世行正直的事，不可随从不义的道路。因为你若行事诚实，你的事务必顺利成全，凡行义的人也是如此。要按你的财物施舍；施舍的时候，眼不可嫉视，脸也不可转离穷人，神的脸就不转离你。你若富足，就按富足施舍；你若只有少许，也不要怕按那少许施舍：因为你为自己积存美好的财宝，以备急难之日。因为施舍能救人脱离死亡，不容他进入黑暗。施舍在至高者面前，对一切施与的人都是美好的礼物。我儿，要谨防一切淫乱的事，首先要娶本族之女为妻，不可娶不属你父支派的外邦女子为妻：因为我们乃是众先知的儿女——挪亚、亚伯拉罕、以撒、雅各。我儿，要记念我们的列祖从起初都娶本族的女子为妻，在他们儿女身上蒙福，他们的后裔必得那地为业。所以，我儿，要爱你的弟兄，不可在心里轻看你本族的儿女，以致不娶他们中间的女子为妻；因为骄傲里有败坏和许多祸患，淫乱里有衰残和极大的穷乏：淫乱是饥荒之母。凡为你作工之人的工钱，不可在你那里过夜，要亲手当日给他：因为你若服事神，他也必报偿你。我儿，你行的一切事都要谨慎，你一切的谈吐都要有智慧。你所恨恶的，不可行在别人身上；不可喝酒至醉；醉酒也不可与你同行在路上。

===Original===

Give of thy bread to the hungry, and of thy garments to them that are naked; and according to thine abundance give alms; and let not thine eye be envious, when thou givest alms. Pour out thy bread on the burial of the just, but give nothing to the wicked. Ask counsel of all that are wise, and despise not any counsel that is profitable. Bless the Lord thy God alway, and desire of him that thy ways may be directed, and that all thy paths and counsels may prosper: for every nation hath not counsel; but the Lord himself giveth all good things, and he humbleth whom he will, as he will; now, therefore, my son, remember my commandments, neither let them be put out of thy mind.

And now I signify this to thee, that I committed ten talents to Gabael the son of Gabrias at Rages in Media. And fear not, my son, that we are made poor: for thou hast much wealth, if thou fear God, and depart from all sin, and do that which is pleasing in his sight.

===Chinese===

要将你的饼分给饥饿的人，将你的衣服给赤身的人；按你的富足施舍，施舍的时候眼不可嫉视。要把你的饼倾在义人的葬礼上，却不可给什么与恶人。要向一切有智慧的人求教，不可轻看任何有益的忠告。要常常称颂主你的神，求他指引你的道路，愿你一切的路径与谋算尽都亨通：因为不是每个国民都有谋略，惟有主自己赐下一切美物，他要卑微谁就卑微谁，随己意而行；所以，我儿，要记念我的诫命，不可让它们离开你的心。

如今我告诉你，我曾把十他连得交给加布利亚的儿子加贝拉，存在玛代的拉吉斯。我儿，不要怕我们贫穷：你若敬畏神，远离一切罪，行他眼中看为喜悦的事，你就有大财。

### V / 五
===Original===

Tobias then answered and said, Father, I will do all things which thou hast commanded me: But how can I receive the money, seeing that I know him not?

Then he gave him the handwriting, and said unto him, Seek thee a man which may go with thee, whiles I yet live, and I will give him wages: and go and receive the money.

Therefore when he went to seek a man, he found Raphael that was an angel. But he knew not; and he said unto him, Canst thou go with me to Rages? and knowest thou those places well?

To whom the angel said, I will go with thee, and I know the way well: for I have lodged with our brother Gabael.

Then Tobias said unto him, Tarry for me, till I tell my father.

Then he said unto him, Go, and tarry not.

So he went in and said to his father, Behold, I have found one which will go with me.

Then he said, Call him unto me, that I may know of what tribe he is, and whether he be a trusty man to go with thee.

So he called him, and he came in, and they saluted one another. Then Tobit said unto him, Brother, show me of what tribe and family thou art.

To whom he said, Dost thou seek for a tribe or family, or an hired man to go with thy son?

Then Tobit said unto him, I would know, brother, thy kindred and name.

Then he said, I am Azarias, the son of Ananias the great, and of thy brethren.

Then Tobit said, Thou art welcome, brother; be not now angry with me because I have enquired to know thy tribe and thy family; for thou art my brother, of an honest and good stock: for I know Ananias and Jonathas, sons of that great Samaias, as we went together to Jerusalem to worship, and offered the firstborn, and the tenths of the fruits; and they were not seduced with the error of our brethren: my brother, thou art of a good stock. But tell me, what wages shall I give thee? wilt thou a drachm a day, and things necessary, as to mine own son? Yea, moreover, if ye return safe, I will add something to thy wages.

So they were well pleased.

Then said he to Tobias, Prepare thyself for the journey, and God send you a good journey.

And when his son had prepared all things for the journey, his father said, Go thou with this man, and God, which dwelleth in heaven, prosper your journey, and the angel of God keep you company.

So they went forth both, and the young man’s dog with them.

===Chinese===

多比雅回答说：父亲啊，凡你所吩咐我的，我都必行；只是那银子我怎能取回来呢？我不认识他呀。

他便把字据交给他，说：你去寻一个能与你同去的人，趁我还活着，我好给他工钱；你去把银子取回来。

他去寻人的时候，遇见了拉斐耳，那是一位天使；他却不知道。他对他说：你能与我同往拉吉斯去么？你熟悉那些地方么？

天使对他说：我愿与你同去，那路我熟悉得很；我曾在我们弟兄加贝拉家里宿过。

多比雅对他说：请等一等，等我告诉我父亲。

他对他说：去吧，不要耽延。

他便进去对父亲说：看哪，我找到了一个愿与我同去的人。

他说：把他叫来见我，我好知道他是哪一支派的，他是不是可靠的人，可以与你同去。

他便去叫他；他进来了，两人彼此问安。托比对他说：弟兄啊，请告诉我，你是哪一支派、哪一族的。

他对他说：你是要问支派家族呢，还是要找一个雇工陪你儿子去呢？

托比对他说：弟兄啊，我愿意知道你的亲族和名字。

他说：我是亚拿尼亚的儿子亚撒利雅，出于那大族，是你弟兄们中间的一个。

托比说：弟兄啊，欢迎你；不要因我询问你的支派家族向我发怒；因为你是我的弟兄，出身高贵良善；我认得亚拿尼亚和约拿单，就是那伟大的撒玛雅的两个儿子；我们曾一同上耶路撒冷去敬拜，献上头生的和果实的十分之一；他们没有随从我们弟兄们的迷惑。我的弟兄，你是好族人家的。请告诉我，我给你什么工钱呢？一天一德拉克马，加上必需的用品，如同我自己的儿子？还有，你们若平安回来，我要在你的工钱之外再加添。

两人都满意了。

他对多比雅说：预备行装上路吧，愿神赐你们好旅程。

他儿子把行路的一切都预备好了，他父亲说：你与这人同去吧；愿住在天上的神使你们的前程顺遂，愿神的使者与你们同行。

于是两人出来了，少年人的狗也随着他们。

===Original===

But Anna his mother wept, and said to Tobit, Why hast thou sent away our son? is he not the staff of our hand, in going in and out before us? Be not greedy to add money to money: but let it be as refuse in respect of our child. For that which the Lord hath given us to live with doth suffice us.

Then said Tobit to her, Take no care, my sister; he shall return in safety, and thine eyes shall see him. For the good angel will keep him company, and his journey shall be prosperous, and he shall return safe.

Then she made an end of weeping.

===Chinese===

他母亲亚拿却哭泣，对托比说：你为什么打发我们的儿子走呢？他岂不是我们在世出入所倚靠的杖么？不要贪心，在钱上加钱；在孩子的份上，钱财只当看作粪土。主所赐给我们养生的一切，于我们已经够了。

托比对她说：妹子啊，不要挂虑；他必平平安安回来，你的眼睛必得再见他。因为善天使必与他同行，他的路程必亨通，他必平安回来。

她这才止住了哭。

### VI / 六
===Original===

And as they went on their journey, they came in the evening to the river Tigris, and they lodged there. And when the young man went down to wash himself, a fish leaped out of the river, and would have devoured him. Then the angel said unto him, Take the fish. And the young man laid hold of the fish, and drew it to land. To whom the angel said, Open the fish, and take the heart and the liver and the gall, and put them up safely.

So the young man did as the angel commanded him; and when they had roasted the fish, they did eat it: then they both went on their way, till they drew near to Ecbatane. Then the young man said to the angel, Brother Azarias, to what use is the heart and the liver and the gall of the fish?

And he said unto him, Touching the heart and the liver, if a devil or an evil spirit trouble any, we must make a smoke thereof before the man or the woman, and the party shall be no more vexed. As for the gall, it is good to anoint a man that hath whiteness in his eyes, and he shall be healed.

And when they were come near to Rages, The angel said to the young man, Brother, today we shall lodge with Raguel, who is thy cousin; he also hath one only daughter, named Sara; I will speak for her, that she may be given thee for a wife. For to thee doth the right of her appertain, seeing thou only art of her kindred. And the maid is fair and wise: now therefore hear me, and I will speak to her father; and when we return from Rages we will celebrate the marriage: for I know that Raguel cannot marry her to another according to the law of Moses, but he shall be guilty of death, because the right of inheritance doth rather appertain to thee than to any other.

Then the young man answered the angel, I have heard, Brother Azarias, that this maid hath been given to seven men, who all died in the marriage chamber. And now I am the only son of my father, and I am afraid, lest, if I go in unto her, I die, as the others before: for a wicked spirit loveth her, which hurteth no body, but those which come unto her; wherefore I also fear lest I die, and bring my father’s and my mother’s life because of me to the grave with sorrow: for they have no other son to bury them.

===Chinese===

他们上路前行，傍晚到了底格里斯河，就在那里过夜。少年人下去洗澡的时候，有一条鱼从河里跳上来，想要吞吃他。天使对他说：抓住那鱼。少年人就抓住那鱼，拖上了岸。天使对他说：剖开鱼，取出鱼心、鱼肝和鱼胆，好好收存。

少年人就照天使所吩咐的行了；他们烤了鱼吃了，又上路前行，直到挨近埃克巴塔纳。少年人对天使说：亚撒利雅弟兄啊，鱼心、鱼肝、鱼胆有什么用处呢？

他对他说：至于鱼心和鱼肝，若有魔鬼或恶灵搅扰什么人，我们只要在那男人或女人面前用它们熏一熏，那受搅扰的就必不再受苦。至于鱼胆，膏在有白翳眼睛的人的眼睛上，他就必痊愈。

他们挨近拉吉斯的时候，天使对少年人说：弟兄啊，今天我们要在拉贵尔那里住宿，他是你的亲戚；他也有一个独生的女儿，名叫撒拉；我要为她提说，使她可以给你为妻。因为娶她的权利原属于你，因为只有你是她的至亲。那女子又美貌又聪明；所以现在听我说，我要向她父亲提说；等我们从拉吉斯回来，就举行婚礼：因为我知道拉贵尔不能照摩西的律法把她嫁给别人，否则他就该治死，因为继承之理原属于你，不属于别人。

少年人回答天使说：亚撒利雅弟兄啊，我听说过，这女子已经许配过七个月人，他们都在洞房里死了。如今我是父亲独生的儿子，我害怕，恐怕我进到她那里，也像以前那些人一样死了；因为有个恶灵爱她，那灵不伤害别人，只伤害到她面前来的人；所以我也怕我死了，叫我父亲母亲因我的缘故，悲伤着下到坟墓里去：因为他们没有别的儿子葬埋他们。

===Original===

Then the angel said unto him, Dost thou not remember the precepts which thy father gave thee, that thou shouldest marry a wife of thine own kindred? wherefore hear me, O my brother; for she shall be given thee to wife; and make no reckoning of the evil spirit; for this same night shall she be given thee in marriage. And when thou shalt come into the marriage chamber, thou shalt take the ashes of perfume, and lay upon them some of the heart and liver of the fish, and shalt make a smoke with it: And the devil shall smell it, and flee away, and never come again any more: but when thou shalt come to her, rise up both of you, and pray to God which is merciful, who will have pity on you, and save you: fear not, for she is appointed unto thee from the beginning: and thou shalt preserve her, and she shall go with thee. Moreover I suppose that she shall bear thee children.

Now when Tobias had heard these things, he loved her, and his heart was effectually joined to her.

===Chinese===

天使对他说：你不记得你父亲所吩咐你的话么，叫你娶本族的女子为妻？所以，我的弟兄啊，听我说：她必给你为妻；不要把那恶灵放在心上；因为今夜她就要许配给你。你进了洞房，要取香料的灰，把鱼心鱼肝放一些在上面，用来熏一熏；那魔鬼闻见那气味，就必逃走，永远不再回来。你到她跟前的时候，你们两人都要起来，呼求怜悯人的神，他必怜恤你们，拯救你们；不要惧怕，因为她从起初就指定给你；你要保爱她，她必与你同行。而且我想，她必为你生儿养女。

多比雅听了这些话，就爱她，他的心切实地系在她身上了。

### VII / 七
===Original===

And when they were come to Ecbatane, they came to the house of Raguel, and Sara met them: and after they had saluted one another, she brought them into the house.

Then said Raguel to Edna his wife. How like is this young man to Tobit my cousin! And Raguel asked them, From whence are ye, brethren?

To whom they said, We are of the sons of Nephthalim, which are captives in Nineve.

Then he said to them, Do ye know Tobit our kinsman?

And they said, We know him.

Then said he, Is he in good health?

And they said, He is both alive, and in good health: and Tobias said, He is my father.

Then Raguel leaped up, and kissed him, and wept, And blessed him, and said unto him, Thou art the son of an honest and good man. But when he had heard that Tobit was blind, he was sorrowful, and wept. And likewise Edna his wife and Sara his daughter wept. Moreover they entertained them cheerfully; and after that they had killed a ram of the flock, they set store of meat on the table.

Then said Tobias to Raphael, Brother Azarias, speak of those things of which thou didst talk in the way, and let this business be dispatched.

So he communicated the matter with Raguel: and Raguel said to Tobias, Eat and drink, and make merry: For it is meet that thou shouldest marry my daughter: nevertheless I will declare unto thee the truth. I have given my daughter in marriage to seven men, who died that night they came in unto her: nevertheless for the present be merry.

But Tobias said, I will eat nothing here, till we agree and swear one to another.

Raguel said, Then take her from henceforth according to the manner, for thou art her cousin, and she is thine, and the merciful God give you good success in all things.

Then he called his daughter Sara, and she came to her father, and he took her by the hand, and gave her to be wife to Tobias, saying, Behold, take her after the law of Moses, and lead her away to thy father. And he blessed them; And called Edna his wife, and took paper, and did write an instrument of covenants, and sealed it.

Then they began to eat. After Raguel called his wife Edna, and said unto her, Sister, prepare another chamber, and bring her in thither. Which when she had done as he had bidden her, she brought her thither:

===Chinese===

他们到了埃克巴塔纳，来到拉贵尔的家；撒拉迎接他们；彼此问安之后，她领他们进屋里去。

拉贵尔对他妻子埃德娜说：这少年人多么像我表亲托比啊！拉贵尔问他们：二位是从哪里来的？

他们说：我们是拿弗他利的子孙，是被掳在尼尼微的。

他对他们说：你们认识我们的亲族托比么？

他们说：我们认识他。

他说：他平安么？

他们说：他活着，也平安。多比雅说：他是我的父亲。

拉贵尔就跳起来，与他亲嘴，哭泣，给他祝福，对他说：你是那诚实良善之人的儿子。他听见托比瞎了眼，就忧伤哭泣；他妻子埃德娜和女儿撒拉也都哭了。他们又快快活活地款待他们；宰了羊群中的一只公绵羊，在桌上摆了许多肉。

多比雅对拉斐耳说：亚撒利雅弟兄啊，说说你路上所提的那件事吧，好把这桩事情办妥了。

他便向拉贵尔说明这事；拉贵尔对多比雅说：吃吧，喝吧，快乐吧；因为你当娶我的女儿；不过我要把实情告诉你。我已经把女儿嫁给七个月人，他们进她房间的那夜都死了；只是眼下你们且欢乐。

多比雅说：不谈妥、彼此起誓，我在这里什么都不吃。

拉贵尔说：那么从此你就娶她吧，照着规矩，因为你是她的亲戚，她是你的；愿怜悯人的神使你们在一切事上顺遂。

他叫了女儿撒拉来；她到父亲跟前，他拉着她的手，把她交给多比雅为妻，说：看哪，照摩西的律法，你娶她吧，领她往你父亲那里去。他就给他们祝福；又叫了妻子埃德娜来，取了纸，写了婚约的文书，盖了印。

他们才开始吃。拉贵尔又叫妻子埃德娜，对她说：妹子啊，预备另一间屋子，领她进去。她照他所吩咐的办了，就把女儿领进去；

===Original===

And she wept, and she received the tears of her daughter, and said unto her, Be of good comfort, my daughter; the Lord of heaven and earth give thee joy for this thy sorrow: be of good comfort, my daughter.

===Chinese===

她哭泣，擦去女儿的眼泪，对她说：女儿啊，放心；愿天地的主使你这忧愁变为喜乐。女儿啊，放心吧。

### VIII / 八
===Original===

And when they had supped, they brought Tobias in unto her. And as he went, he remembered the words of Raphael, and took the ashes of the perfumes, and put the heart and the liver of the fish thereupon, and made a smoke therewith. The which smell when the evil spirit had smelled, he fled into the utmost parts of Egypt, and the angel bound him.

And after that they were both shut in together, Tobias rose out of the bed, and said, Sister, arise, and let us pray that God would have pity on us. Then began Tobias to say, Blessed art thou, O God of our fathers, and blessed is thy holy and glorious name forever; let the heavens bless thee, and all thy creatures. Thou madest Adam, and gavest him Eve his wife for an helper and stay: of them came mankind: thou hast said, It is not good that man should be alone; let us make unto him an aid like unto himself. And now, O Lord, I take not this my sister for lust, but uprightly: therefore mercifully ordain that we may become aged together.

And she said with him, Amen.

So they slept both that night.

And Raguel arose, and went and made a grave, Saying, I fear lest he also be dead. But when Raguel was come into his house, He said unto his wife Edna, Send one of the maids, and let her see whether he be alive: if he be not, that we may bury him and no man know it.

So the maid opened the door, and went in, and found them both asleep, and came forth, and told them that he was alive.

Then Raguel praised God, and said, O God, thou art worthy to be praised with all pure and holy praise; therefore let thy saints praise thee with all thy creatures; and let all thine angels and thine elect praise thee forever. Thou art to be praised, for thou hast made me joyful; and that is not come to me which I suspected; but thou hast dealt with us according to thy great mercy. Thou art to be praised, because thou hast had mercy of two that were the only begotten children of their fathers: grant them mercy, O Lord, and finish their life in health with joy and mercy.

Then Raguel bade his servants to fill the grave.

===Chinese===

他们吃过晚饭，就把多比雅领到她那里去。他进去的时候，记起拉斐耳的话，取了香料的灰，把鱼心鱼肝放在上面，用它熏了熏。恶灵闻见那气味，就逃往埃及极边之地；天使把他捆绑了。

他们两人单独关在屋里之后，多比雅从床上起来，说：妹妹啊，起来吧，我们祷告，求神怜恤我们。多比雅开始祷告说：我们列祖的神啊，你是应当称颂的，你圣洁荣耀的名永受称颂，直到永远；愿诸天称颂你，愿你一切所造的都称颂你。你造了亚当，又为他造了妻子夏娃，作他的帮助与依靠；从他们生出了人类；你曾说过：人独居不好，我们要为他造一个与他相似的帮助。主啊，如今我娶我这个妹妹，不是出于情欲，乃是正直地娶她；求你怜悯，安排我们可以一同白头到老。

她与他说：阿们。

那一夜两人都睡了。

拉贵尔起来，去掘了一座坟，说：恐怕他也死了。拉贵尔回到家里，对妻子埃德娜说：打发一个使女去看看，他到底活没活着；若不活着，我们好把他葬埋了，不叫人知道。

使女开了门，进去，见两人都睡着，出来告诉他们说他活着。

拉贵尔就称颂神，说：神啊，你配得一切纯洁圣洁的赞美；愿你的圣徒同你一切所造的一同赞美你；愿你众天使和你选民永远赞美你。你配受赞美，因为你使我喜乐；我所料想的事没有临到我，你却照你的大怜悯待了我们。你配受赞美，因为你怜恤了两个都是父亲独生的孩子；主啊，求你赐他们怜悯，愿他们在健康、喜乐与怜悯中走完他们的一生。

拉贵尔吩咐仆人们把坟墓填平了。

===Original===

And he kept the wedding feast fourteen days. For before the days of the marriage were finished, Raguel had said unto him by an oath, that he should not depart till the fourteen days of the marriage were expired; and then he should take the half of his goods, and go in safety to his father; and should have the rest when I and my wife be dead.

===Chinese===

他又设婚宴十四天；因为在婚期未满之先，拉贵尔已经指着誓言对他说，不到十四天的婚期届满，不许他离开；然后他可以取他一半的财物，平平安安回父亲那里去；等我与我妻子死后再得其余的。

### IX / 九
===Original===

Then Tobias called Raphael, and said unto him, Brother Azarias, take with thee a servant, and two camels, and go to Rages of Media to Gabael, and bring me the money, and bring him to the wedding. For Raguel hath sworn that I shall not depart. But my father counteth the days; and if I tarry long, he will be very sorry.

So Raphael went out, and lodged with Gabael, and gave him the handwriting: who brought forth bags which were sealed up, and gave them to him. And early in the morning they went forth both together, and came to the wedding: and Tobias blessed his wife.

===Chinese===

多比雅叫了拉斐耳来，对他说：亚撒利雅弟兄啊，你带上一个仆人、两匹骆驼，往玛代的拉吉斯去见加贝拉，把银子给我取来，也把他带来赴婚宴。因为拉贵尔起誓，不许我离开。只是我父亲在数算日子；我若耽延久了，他必非常忧愁。

拉斐耳便出去，宿在加贝拉那里，把字据交给他；加贝拉取出封好的口袋，交给了他。第二天一早，两人一同起来，赴婚宴去了；多比雅给他的妻子祝福。

### X / 十
===Original===

Now Tobit his father counted every day: and when the days of the journey were expired, and they came not. Then Tobit said, Are they detained? or is Gabael dead, and there is no man to give him the money? Therefore he was very sorry.

Then his wife said unto him, My son is dead, seeing that he stayeth long; and she began to bewail him, and said, Now I care for nothing, my son, since I have let thee go, the light of mine eyes.

To whom Tobit said, Hold thy peace, take no care, for he is safe.

But she said, Hold thy peace, and deceive me not; my son is dead.

And she went out every day into the way which they went, and did eat no meat in the daytime, and ceased not to bewail her son Tobias, until the fourteen days of the wedding were expired, which Raguel had sworn that he should spend there.

Then Tobias said to Raguel, Let me go, for my father and my mother look no more to see me.

But his father in law said unto him, Tarry with me, and I will send to thy father, and they shall declare unto him how things go with thee.

But Tobias said, No; but let me go to my father.

Then Raguel arose, and gave him Sara his wife, and half his goods, servants, and cattle, and money: And he blessed them, and sent them away, saying, The God of heaven give you a prosperous journey, my children. And he said to his daughter, Honour thy father and thy mother in law, which are now thy parents, that I may hear good report of thee. And he kissed her.

Edna also said to Tobias, The Lord of heaven restore thee, my dear brother, and grant that I may see thy children of my daughter Sara before I die, that I may rejoice before the Lord: behold I commit my daughter unto thee of special trust; wherefore do not entreat her evil.

===Chinese===

他父亲托比天天数算日子；行期的日子满了，他们还没有回来。托比说：他们是受了拦阻呢？还是加贝拉死了，没有人给他银子呢？他因此非常忧愁。

他妻子对他说：我儿子死了，所以他耽延这么久；她开始为他哀哭，说：我的儿啊，自从我放你走了，你是我眼里的光，如今我什么都不管了。

托比对她说：安静，不要挂虑，他是平安的。

她说：你安静，不要哄我；我儿子死了。

她天天出去，到他们所走的那条路上张望，白日不吃饭，不住地为儿子多比雅哀哭，直到拉贵尔起誓要他在那里度过的十四天婚宴满了。

多比雅对拉贵尔说：让我走吧，因为我父母不再指望见我了。

他岳父对他说：住在我这里吧，我要打发人去告诉你父亲，把你的情形报给他。

多比雅说：不必了；还是让我回父亲那里去吧。

拉贵尔起来，把女儿撒拉给他为妻，又给他一半家产——仆人、牛羊和银钱；给他们祝福，打发他们走，说：愿天上的神赐你们一路平安，我的孩子们。又对女儿说：去孝敬你的公公婆婆，他们如今就是你的父母；愿我听见你的好名声。他与她亲嘴。

埃德娜也对多比雅说：亲爱的弟兄啊，愿天上的主眷顾你，愿我在未死之前，得见我女儿撒拉为你生的儿女，好叫我在主面前欢乐。看哪，我把女儿特别托付给你；所以不可苦待她。

### XI / 十一
===Original===

After these things Tobias went his way, praising God that he had given him a prosperous journey, and blessed Raguel and Edna his wife, and went on his way till they drew near unto Nineve.

Then Raphael said to Tobias, Thou knowest, brother, how thou didst leave thy father: Let us haste before thy wife, and prepare the house. And take in thine hand the gall of the fish.

So they went their way, and the dog went after them.

Now Anna sat looking about toward the way for her son. And when she espied him coming, she said to his father, Behold, thy son cometh, and the man that went with him.

Then said Raphael, I know, Tobias, that thy father will open his eyes. Therefore anoint thou his eyes with the gall, and being pricked therewith, he shall rub, and the whiteness shall fall away, and he shall see thee.

Then Anna ran forth, and fell upon the neck of her son, and said unto him, Seeing I have seen thee, my son, from henceforth I am content to die. And they wept both.

Tobit also went forth toward the door, and stumbled: but his son ran unto him, And took hold of his father: and he strake of the gall on his father’s eyes, saying, Be of good hope, my father.

And when his eyes began to smart, he rubbed them; And the whiteness pilled away from the corners of his eyes: and when he saw his son, he fell upon his neck. And he wept, and said, Blessed art thou, O God, and blessed is thy name forever; and blessed are all thine holy angels; For thou hast scourged, and hast taken pity on me: for, behold, I see my son Tobias.

And his son went in rejoicing, and told his father the great things that had happened to him in Media.

Then Tobit went out to meet his daughter in law at the gate of Nineve, rejoicing, and praising God: and they which saw him go marvelled, because he had received his sight. But Tobit gave thanks before them, because God had mercy on him. And when he came near to Sara his daughter in law, he blessed her, saying, Thou art welcome, daughter: God be blessed, which hath brought thee unto us, and blessed be thy father and thy mother.

And there was joy among all his brethren which were at Nineve. And Achiarcharus, and Nasbas his brother’s son, came: And Tobias’ wedding was kept seven days with great joy.

===Chinese===

这些事以后，多比雅上路前行，称颂神赐他顺遂的旅程；又祝福拉贵尔和他妻子埃德娜，继续赶路，直到挨近尼尼微。

拉斐耳对多比雅说：弟兄啊，你知道你离开父亲时他是怎样的；让我们赶在你妻子前头先走，把屋子预备好；你手里要拿着鱼胆。

他们上了路，狗跟在他们后头。

亚拿正坐着朝那条路上张望，等她儿子。她远远望见他来了，就对孩子的父亲说：看哪，你的儿子来了，同他一起的那人也来了。

拉斐耳说：多比雅，我知道你父亲必将睁开眼睛。所以你拿鱼胆膏他的眼睛；膏了叫他刺一刺，他就会揉，白翳就脱落，他就看见你了。

亚拿跑出来，扑在儿子颈项上，对他说：我儿啊，我既亲眼看见了你，从今往后我就是死也甘心了。两人都哭了。

托比也朝门口走出来，绊了一跤；他儿子跑过来扶住父亲，把鱼胆抹在父亲眼睛上，说：父亲啊，要存指望。

他的眼睛开始发痛，他揉了揉；白翳从眼角上脱落下来；他看见儿子，就扑在他颈项上。他哭泣着说：神啊，你是应当称颂的，你的名永受称颂，你众圣天使也都可以称颂；因为你鞭打了我，又怜恤了我；看哪，我看见我的儿子多比雅了。

他儿子欢欢喜喜进去，把他在玛代所遇的大事告诉了父亲。

托比出到尼尼微城门口迎接儿妇，欢欢喜喜称颂神；看见他的人都诧奇，因为他重见了光明。托比在他们面前称谢，因为神怜恤了他。他挨近儿妇撒拉，给她祝福，说：女儿啊，欢迎你；愿神受称颂，因为他把你领到我们这里来；愿你的父亲母亲蒙福。

那时他在尼尼微的众弟兄都欢喜。阿希加和他兄弟的儿子拿斯巴也来了；多比雅的婚宴大大欢乐地摆了七天。

### XII / 十二
===Original===

Then Tobit called his son Tobias, and said unto him, My son, see that the man have his wages, which went with thee, and thou must give him more.

And Tobias said unto him, O father, it is no harm to me to give him half of those things which I have brought: For he hath brought me again to thee in safety, and made whole my wife, and brought me the money, and likewise healed thee.

Then the old man said, It is due unto him.

So he called the angel, and he said unto him, Take half of all that ye have brought, and go away in safety.

Then he took them both apart, and said unto them, Bless God, praise him, and magnify him, and praise him for the things which he hath done unto you in the sight of all that live. It is good to praise God, and exalt his name, and honourably to show forth the works of God; therefore be not slack to praise him. It is good to keep close the secret of a king, but it is honourable to reveal the works of God. Do that which is good, and no evil shall touch you. Prayer is good with fasting and alms and righteousness. A little with righteousness is better than much with unrighteousness. It is better to give alms than to lay up gold: For alms doth deliver from death, and shall purge away all sin. Those that exercise alms and righteousness shall be filled with life; But they that sin are enemies to their own life. Surely I will keep close nothing from you. For I said, It was good to keep close the secret of a king, but that it was honourable to reveal the works of God. Now therefore, when thou didst pray, and Sara thy daughter in law, I did bring the remembrance of your prayers before the Holy One: and when thou didst bury the dead, I was with thee likewise. And when thou didst not delay to rise up, and leave thy dinner, to go and cover the dead, thy good deed was not hid from me: but I was with thee. And now God hath sent me to heal thee and Sara thy daughter in law. I am Raphael, one of the seven holy angels, which present the prayers of the saints, and which go in and out before the glory of the Holy One.

Then they were both troubled, and fell upon their faces: for they feared.

===Chinese===

托比叫他儿子多比雅来，对他说：我儿，要留意把与你们同行那人的工钱给他，并且要多给他。

多比雅对他说：父亲啊，把我所带来的财物分一半给他，于我也无损；因为他使我平安回到你这里，治好了我的妻子，为我取来银子，也照样医治了你。

老人说：我儿，这是他当得的。

他便叫了天使来，对他说：把你所带来的拿一半去，平平安安走吧。

他却把两人带到一边，对他们说：要称颂神，赞美他，尊他为大，在一切活人眼前，为他向你们所行的事赞美他。歌颂神、高举他的名、可敬地宣扬神的作为，是美善的；所以不要怠慢赞美他。保守君王的秘密是好的，但宣扬神的作为是可敬的。行善事，恶就不能害你们。祷告与禁食、施舍、公义并行，是好的。少而有义，胜过多而不义。施舍胜于积攒金银：因为施舍救人脱离死亡，能洗净一切罪。行施舍与公义的人必得生命充满；犯罪的人却是自己性命的仇敌。我必不向你们隐瞒什么。我说过：保守君王的秘密是好的，但宣扬神的作为是可敬的。如今，当你和你的儿妇撒拉祷告的时候，我把你们祷告的记念带到圣者面前；你埋葬死人的时候，我也同样与你同在；你不迟延起来、撇下饭食去掩埋死人的时候，你那善行没有向我隐藏：我与你同在。如今神差我来医治你和你的儿妇撒拉。我是拉斐耳，七位圣天使中的一位，将圣徒的祷告呈到圣者面前，在圣者的荣耀前进出。

两人都惊惶，俯伏在地；因为他们惧怕。

===Original===

But he said unto them, Fear not, for it shall go well with you; praise God therefore. For not of any favour of mine, but by the will of our God I came; wherefore praise him forever. All these days I did appear unto you; but I did neither eat nor drink, but ye did see a vision. Now therefore give God thanks: for I go up to him that sent me; but write all these things which are done in a book.

And when they arose, they saw him no more. Then they confessed the great and wonderful works of God, and how the angel of the Lord had appeared unto them.

===Chinese===

他对他们说：不要惧怕，你们必平安；所以当称颂神。我来，不是出于我的恩惠，乃是凭我们神的旨意；所以当永远赞美他。这些日子我显现与你们；我却不吃也不喝，你们看见的乃是异象。如今要称谢神；我要升到差我来的那里去；你们要把这一切所行的事写在书上。

他们起来，就再见不到他了。他们便称颂神，述说他伟大奇妙的作为，述说主的使者怎样向他们显现。

### XIII / 十三
===Original===

Then Tobit wrote a prayer of rejoicing, and said, Blessed be God that liveth forever, and blessed be his kingdom. For he doth scourge and hath mercy: he leadeth down to hell, and bringeth up again; neither is there any that can avoid his hand. Confess him before the Gentiles, ye children of Israel: for he hath scattered us among them. There declare his greatness, and extol him before all the living: for he is our Lord, and he is the God our Father forever. And he will scourge us for our iniquities, and will have mercy again, and will gather us out of all nations, among whom he hath scattered us. If ye turn to him with your whole heart, and with your whole mind, and deal uprightly before him, then will he turn unto you, and will not hide his face from you. Therefore see what he will do with you, and confess him with your whole mouth, and praise the Lord of might, and extol the everlasting King. In the land of my captivity do I praise him, and declare his might and majesty to a sinful nation. O ye sinners, turn and do justice before him: who can tell if he will accept you, and have mercy on you? I will extol my God, and my soul shall praise the King of heaven, and shall rejoice in his greatness. Let all men speak, and let all praise him for his righteousness.

O Jerusalem, the holy city, he will scourge thee for thy children’s works, and will have mercy again on the sons of the righteous. Give praise to the Lord, for he is good: and praise the everlasting King, that his tabernacle may be builded in thee again with joy, and let him make joyful there in thee those that are captives, and love in thee forever those that are miserable. Many nations shall come from far to the name of the Lord God with gifts in their hands, even gifts to the King of heaven; all generations shall praise thee with great joy. Cursed are all they which hate thee, and blessed shall all be which love thee forever. Rejoice and be glad for the children of the just: for they shall be gathered together, and shall bless the Lord of the just. O blessed are they which love thee, for they shall rejoice in thy peace: blessed are they which have been sorrowful for all thy scourges; for they shall rejoice for thee, when they have seen all thy glory, and shall be glad forever. Let my soul bless God the great King.

===Chinese===

托比于是写下称颂的祷告，说：永活的神是应当称颂的，他的国也当受称颂；因为他鞭打，也施怜悯；他领人下到阴间，也领上来；没有能逃脱他手的。以色列的众子啊，要在列国面前承认他；因为他使我们在他们中间被赶散。要在那里述说他的伟大，在一切活人面前高举他；因为他是我们的主，他是我们的神，是我们永远的父亲。他必因我们的罪孽鞭打我们，又必再施怜恤，把我们从他被赶散到的万国中聚集回来。你们若尽心尽意归向他，在他面前行事正直，他就必转向你们，不向你掩面。所以现在要看他向你们怎样行，要全口承认他，称颂大能的主，高举永世的君王。我在被掳之地称颂他，向犯罪的国民述说他的大能与威严。罪人啊，回转吧，在他面前行公义；谁知道他必悦纳你们、怜恤你们呢？我要高举我的神，我的魂要称颂天上的王，要因他的伟大欢欣。让众人都述说，让人人因他的公义赞美他。

耶路撒冷啊，圣城啊，他必因你儿女的行为鞭打你，又必再怜恤义人的子孙。要称颂主，因为他是美善的；要赞美永世的君王，愿他的帐幕喜乐地在你中间重新建立；愿他使你中间被掳的人欢乐，使困苦的人永远爱你。必有列国从远方奉主神的名而来，手里拿着礼物，就是献给天上之王的礼物；万代都要大大喜乐地赞美你。凡恨你的都必受咒诅，凡爱你的必永远蒙福。当为义人的儿女欢喜快乐，因为他们必聚集一处，称颂义人之主。那爱你的有福了，因为他们必因你的平安欢乐；那为你一切的鞭打过忧伤的人有福了，因为他们必因你欢乐——等他们看见你一切的荣耀，便永远喜乐。愿我的魂称颂神，那伟大的王。

===Original===

For Jerusalem shall be built up with sapphires, and emeralds, and precious stone: thy walls and towers and battlements with pure gold. And the streets of Jerusalem shall be paved with beryl and carbuncle and stones of Ophir. And all her streets shall say, Alleluia; and they shall praise him, saying, Blessed be God, which hath extolled it forever.

===Chinese===

因为耶路撒冷必用蓝宝石、绿宝石和宝石建造；你的城墙、城楼与城堞用纯金；耶路撒冷的街道必铺上绿玉、红玉与俄斐的石头。城中一切的街道必说：哈利路亚；他们必赞美他，说：永远高举它的神，是应当称颂的。

### XIV / 十四
===Original===

So Tobit made an end of praising God.

And he was eight and fifty years old when he lost his sight, which was restored to him after eight years: and he gave alms, and he increased in the fear of the Lord God, and praised him.

And when he was very aged, he called his son, and the six sons of his son, and said to him, My son, take thy children; for, behold, I am aged, and am ready to depart out of this life. Go into Media, my son, for I surely believe those things which Jonas the prophet spake of Nineve, that it shall be overthrown; and that for a time peace shall rather be in Media; and that our brethren shall lie scattered in the earth from that good land: and Jerusalem shall be desolate, and the house of God in it shall be burned, and shall be desolate for a time; And that again God will have mercy on them, and bring them again into the land, where they shall build a temple, but not like to the first, until the time of that age be fulfilled; and afterward they shall return from all places of their captivity, and build up Jerusalem gloriously, and the house of God shall be built in it forever with a glorious building, as the prophets have spoken thereof. And all nations shall turn, and fear the Lord God truly, and shall bury their idols. So shall all nations praise the Lord, and his people shall confess God, and the Lord shall exalt his people; and all those which love the Lord God in truth and justice shall rejoice, showing mercy to our brethren. And now, my son, depart out of Nineve, because that those things which the prophet Jonas spake shall surely come to pass. But keep thou the law and the commandments, and show thyself merciful and just, that it may go well with thee. And bury me decently, and thy mother with me; but tarry no longer at Nineve. Remember, my son, how Aman handled Achiarcharus that brought him up, how out of light he brought him into darkness, and how he rewarded him again: yet Achiarcharus was saved, but the other had his reward: for he went down into darkness. Manasses gave alms, and escaped the snares of death which they had set for him: but Aman fell into the snare, and perished. Wherefore now, my son, consider what alms doeth, and how righteousness doth deliver.

===Chinese===

托比就这样结束了对神的称颂。

他失明的时候五十八岁，八年之后重见光明；他行施舍，且越发敬畏主神，称颂他。

他年纪老迈的时候，叫了他儿子和他儿子的六个儿子来，对他说：我儿啊，带上你的儿女；看哪，我已年迈，就要离开今生了。我儿啊，你往玛代去吧，因为我深信先知约拿论尼尼微所说的话必成就，那城必倾覆；一时之间，玛代反倒有平安；我们的弟兄必从那美地上分散在地上各处；耶路撒冷必荒凉，在它里面的神的家必被火焚烧，荒凉一段时间；神必再怜恤他们，领他们再回那地，他们要在那里建造殿宇，却不像头一座，直到那时代的日期满足；此后他们必从被掳的各地归回，荣耀地重建耶路撒冷，神的殿必在其中建造，正如先知们论到的那样，成为荣耀的建筑，直到永远。列国都必回转，真心敬畏主神，埋葬他们的偶像。万民必都称颂主，他的百姓必承认神；主必高举他的民；凡真心实意爱主神的，必在公义中欢乐，向我们的弟兄施怜悯。所以，我儿啊，如今要离开尼尼微，因为先知约拿所说的事必定应验。你要谨守律法和诫命，显出怜悯与公义，好叫你凡事亨通。要体体面面地葬埋我，也葬埋你母亲；在尼尼微不要再停留了。我儿啊，要记念亚曼怎样对待抚养他的阿希加，怎样叫他出黑暗入光明，亚曼后来又怎样遭了报应；只是阿希加得了拯救，那另一个人得了报应：因为他下到黑暗里去了。玛拿西施行施舍，逃脱了人给他设下的死亡网罗；亚曼却落入网罗灭亡了。所以，我儿啊，如今要思想施舍有什么果效，公义怎样救人。

===Original===

When he had said these things, he gave up the ghost in the bed, being an hundred and eight and fifty years old; and he buried him honourably. And when Anna his mother was dead, he buried her with his father.

But Tobias departed with his wife and children to Ecbatane to Raguel his father in law, Where he became old with honour, and he buried his father and mother in law honourably, and he inherited their substance, and his father Tobit’s.

And he died at Echatane in Media, being an hundred and seven and twenty years old. But before he died he heard of the destruction of Nineve, which was taken by Nabuchodonosor and Assuerus: and before his death he rejoiced over Nineve.

===Chinese===

他说完这些话，就在床上断了气，享年一百五十八岁；他儿子体体面面地葬埋了他。他母亲亚拿死后，他也把她与他父亲葬在一处。

多比雅带着妻子儿女往埃克巴塔纳他岳父拉贵尔那里去了；他在那里年老得享尊荣，体体面面地葬埋了岳父岳母，继承了他们的产业，也继承了他父亲托比的产业。

他死在玛代的埃克巴塔纳，享年一百二十七岁。他未死之先，听见了尼尼微倾覆的消息——那城是被尼布甲尼撒和亚哈随鲁攻取的；他临终之前，为尼尼微的灭亡欢喜。

## Author’s Note / 作者附记
===Original===

A rereading of the Apocrypha, while I was living in Kanto, Manchuria, some years ago, seemed to me to show a curiously exact parallel between the position of the exiled Jews of Tobit’s day and that of the exiled White Russians in ours. Even most of the details of Tobit’s story it seemed to me, might be read as referring, without irrelevance or even improbability to the adventures of a White Russian refugee family. I am therefore very anxious that any reader of this book should keep, as it were, one eye on the Apocrypha, and, for this reason, I have included a complete copy of Tobit at the end of my book. I have not added Judith, since she makes but a shadowy and vicarious appearance in my story.

It is difficult for me now to read the Book of Tobit with an impartial eye. Perhaps over-frequent rereadings have thrown the book rather out of focus in my view, and perhaps I have identified too elaborately my Russians with the ancient Jews. Certainly the rather complacent narrative of old Tobit leaves me with the impression that he wrote down the experiences of his family as he would have liked them to happen, rather than as they did happen. Tobit’s partly tacit insistence on the *I-told-you-so* and *father-always-knows-best* motifs, suggests a wistfully self-compensating diary, rather than an impersonal record of facts, and his conception of his son’s exclusively filial orientation makes a modern reader sceptical as to whether a few thousand years could make so much difference to youthful human nature as all that. The Book of Tobit seems to me to give, in fact, purely Tobit’s side of what must have been a many-sided story. Old Tobit was the center of his picture, as Old Sergei in my book would have liked to be the center of his. My narrative discounts this patriarchal bias.

With regard to the setting of my story, my friend, the late Sir Valentine Chirol, and my husband, J. C. O’G. Anderson, have kindly supplied me with facts from their superior knowledge, to supplement and explain my own ignorant observations of the bewildering confusion of tongues and nationalities in the midst of which I have set my scene. This confusion is a commonplace of life in Manchuria, but may seem fantastic to readers unfamiliar with that part of the world.

===Chinese===

几年前我住在满洲关东的时候，重读了一次《次经》，觉得托比时代被掳的犹太人，同我们这个时代被放逐的白俄，处境之间有一种出奇精确的平行。甚至托比故事里的大部分细节，在我看来，都可以读作对一个白俄难民家庭种种遭遇的影射，既不牵强，也不失情理。所以我非常希望本书的读者，读书时仿佛拿一只眼睛盯着《次经》；为这个缘故，我在书末附了《多比传》全文。我没有附《犹滴传》，因为她在我这个故事里只有一场影影绰绰的替身戏。

如今要我以无偏的眼睛读《多比传》，已经很难了。也许重读得太勤，把这书在我眼里读得走了焦；也许我把我的俄国人同古代犹太人认得太细。老托比那篇颇为自得的记述，固然留给我一个印象：他写下的是他家里人种种经历中他巴望发生的样子，而不是实际发生的样子。托比半明半暗地死咬着“我早说过”同“父亲永远最懂”这两个母题，叫人觉得那更像一部聊以自慰的日记，而不是一份不动感情的实录；他心目中儿子那份纯粹的孝子定向，也叫现代读者起疑：几千年光阴，究竟能不能把青年人性改变到那个地步。在我看来，《多比传》所给的，事实上纯粹是托比的一面之词——那故事本必有好几个侧面。老托比是他那幅画的中心，正像本书里的老谢尔盖巴望做他自己那幅画的中心。我这个叙述，把这份父权的偏心折了价。

至于我故事的背景，我的朋友、已故的瓦伦丁·奇罗尔爵士，同我的丈夫J. C. 奥格·安德森，都曾以他们远胜于我的知识，好意供给过我许多事实，来补充并解释我自己对那片言语与民族的大混乱的无知观察——我的场景就设在那片混乱当中。这种混乱在满洲是生活的家常便饭，但对不熟悉世界那一角的读者，也许显得离奇。

===Original===

Kanto, the part of Manchuria in which my Malinins live, is the Japanese name for a territory about the size of Wales, in the most southerly corner of the east side of Manchuria. To the south of it is Korea, to the east﻿—shutting it off from the sea﻿—is the Maritime Province of Russian Siberia and Vladivostok. To the north and to the west is the rest of Manchuria.

Kanto, though a part of Chinese Manchuria, is chiefly populated by Koreans. The next most important element numerically is the Chinese; then come Japanese, and finally a sprinkling of Russians. I do not here include the few European and American missionaries, who are not permanent residents.

In the days when Korea was a tribute-paying dependency of China, the exact frontier between Korea and Chinese Manchuria was not a matter of great importance, and was probably never very clearly defined. In 1909, however, with the Japanese annexation of Korea﻿—of 1910﻿—in sight, China and Japan signed the Chientao (or Kanto) Agreement, determining what would shortly be the frontier between the two countries. This agreement awarded Kanto to China, although Koreans constituted an enormous majority of the population. The reason for this seemingly illogical award was doubtless that the T’umen River, to the north of which Kanto lies, offered an excellent natural boundary line from the military and geographical points of view, ethnological claims not having in those days the weight which they have since acquired. The decisions of governments did not affect﻿—and have not affected to this day﻿—the development of the soil of Kanto by Koreans. The fertility of this soil, and the prosperity of the original Korean residents of this region, have attracted, and continue to attract, large numbers of immigrants from the south bank of the river﻿—i.e., from Korea proper.

===Chinese===

关东，我的马利宁一家所住的这块满洲地方，是个日本名字，指一块大约威尔士那么大的地方，在满洲东侧最南的角上。它南面是朝鲜；东面——把它同海隔开——是俄国西伯利亚的滨海省和符拉迪沃斯托克。北面和西面是满洲其余的地方。

关东虽是中国满洲的一部分，居民却主要是朝鲜人。数目上次一位的要紧成分是中国人；再次是日本人；末了是零星几处俄国人。这里不算那少数欧美传教士，他们不算长住居民。

在朝鲜还是中国朝贡藩属的年代，朝鲜同中国满洲之间的确切边界不是什么大事，多半也从来没有十分划清过。可是到了一九〇九年，日本吞并朝鲜（一九一〇年）已经在望，中日两国便签了《间岛（关东）协约》，划定不久之后的两国边界。这条协约把关东判给了中国，尽管居民里朝鲜人占压倒的多数。这个貌似不合逻辑的判决，理由无疑在于：关东所倚的图们江，从军事和地理的观点看，是一条极好的天然界线；而在那个年代，民族学的权利还没有后来那样重的分量。政府的这些决定，不曾影响——至今也不曾影响——朝鲜人对关东土地的开垦。这片土地的肥沃，同这个地区原有朝鲜居民的兴旺，一直吸引着、而且还在吸引着大批移民从河的南岸过来——也就是从朝鲜本土过来。

===Original===

The fact of Chinese sovereignty sufficiently accounts for the presence of Chinese in Kanto. But here, as nowhere else in Manchuria, they are in a minority of the resident population. What Chinese there are in Kanto have not settled to any considerable extent on the land; most of them live there as territorial officials, merchants, and middlemen, or travel there as agents and peddlers, and are content to leave the bulk of the actual development of the soil to the Koreans. The million and more of Chinese immigrants who pour into other parts of Manchuria every year, mainly from the Shantung and Hopeh provinces, have not as yet penetrated in any great numbers to this remote corner, Kanto, and are still being absorbed by the vast fertile tracts of undeveloped land to the west and north, which are, to them, nearer home. It is to be noted, too, that much the most direct approach to Kanto from China proper is by rail through Korea﻿—which is now a foreign country to Chinese. The only other approaches are from the north, and consist of very rough cart roads through unsettled and desolate country.

The absence, or apparent absence, of Manchus in what would seem to be their native land, Manchuria, is explained by the fact that, although they were at one time conquerors of China, China’s superior civilization has conquered her conquerors; the Manchus adopted in a great measure Chinas language and customs, and have become﻿—except in a few remote regions﻿—almost indistinguishable from Chinese. In their own land, Manchuria, they have been swamped by the flood of immigrants from China proper. In this note and in the story, then, the word Chinese includes Manchus.

===Chinese===

中国拥有主权这一事实，足以说明关东为什么有中国人。可是在这里——同满洲任何地方都不同——他们在常住人口里是少数。关东的中国人并没有在土地上大量定居；他们多数是作为地方官吏、商人、中介住在那里，或者作为代理人和货郎往来奔波，乐得把实际开垦土地的担子大半留给朝鲜人。每年从山东、河北等省涌进满洲其他地方的一百多万中国移民，至今还没有大批渗到关东这个偏远的角落，仍旧被西边和北边大片大片肥沃的未开垦地吸收着——那对他们离家更近。还应注意，从中国本部进关东，最直接的路径是取道朝鲜的铁路——朝鲜如今对中国人是一个外国了。其余的路只有从北面来，都是些穿过没有人烟的荒地的、非常难走的大车道。

满洲本是满人的本土，这里却看不见满人（或者说似乎看不见），其解释是：他们虽一度征服过中国，中国较高的文明又征服了他们的征服者；满人在很大程度上采用了中国的语言与习俗，除了少数偏远地区，几乎同中国人分不出来。在他们自己的土地满洲，他们已被中国本部涌来的移民大潮淹没。所以，在这篇附记同这个故事里，“中国人”一词包括满人在内。

===Original===

The Japanese, being now the overlords of Korea, are near neighbors of Kanto. Hence Japanese consulates, hospitals, and schools, with their appropriate staffs, are to be found in the towns of Kanto, and there are also Japanese mining and railway concessionaires, and considerable numbers of Japanese traders. The fact of Japanese police functioning in this district is more difficult to explain, but the following points may be noted as having a bearing on this anomaly. Japan has fought two life-and-death wars in Manchuria, and expended enormous sums of money on railway and other development there; in addition to the extraterritorial rights which her nationals﻿—in common with the British, French, Americans, etc.﻿—still enjoy in China, she claims special privileges in Manchuria and Kanto (which are in some cases contested by the Chinese), arising partly out of her succession to the privileged position which the Russians held in Manchuria before the Russo-Japanese war, and partly out of subsequent Sino-Japanese conventions and agreements; Korean “independence” movements have originated in Kanto in the past, and it is known that the communistic doctrines which the Japanese view with such suspicion have made some headway among Kanto Koreans. Finally, the nationality﻿—Chinese or Japanese﻿—of Korean residents in Kanto is not infrequently in dispute.

===Chinese===

日本人如今既是朝鲜的主宰，也就是关东的近邻。因此关东各镇上有日本的领事馆、医院、学校，连同各自的职员；还有日本的矿权、铁路权的承租人，以及相当数目的日本商人。日本警察在这个地区行使职权，这一事实较难解释，但以下几点或与此反常现象有关，值得一提。日本在满洲打过两场生死攸关的战争，在铁路及其他开发上花过巨额款项；除了她的国民——同英、法、美等国国民一样——至今在中国享有的治外法权之外，她还要求在满洲和关东的特权（有些场合为中国所争执）：一部分承自日俄战争前俄国人在满洲的特权地位，一部分出自后来中日之间的各公约与协定；朝鲜的“独立”运动过去发源于关东；而且人所共知，日本人那样疑忌的共产主义学说，在关东的朝鲜人中间颇有进展。最后，关东的朝鲜居民究竟属中国籍还是日本籍，也常常是件争执不休的事。

===Original===

There have always been a certain number of Russians in Manchuria. Imperialist Russia long cherished designs on this rich and handy country, and even after these hopes were overthrown by the Russian defeat at the hands of the Japanese, Russian official establishments were maintained in many centers in Manchuria and Kanto. The Russian revolution of 1917 cut off all officials of the Imperial regime from their sources of authority, and these families, for lack of anywhere else to go to, continued to live in Manchuria, as in other parts of China, living as best they might on their savings. These exiles were joined, little by little, by the more timid or foreseeing among White Russians already in Siberia. Among such early refugees I assume my Malinins and Ostapenkos to be numbered. These isolated exiles were presently swamped by a flood of White Russian fugitives﻿—the remnants of a defeated army accompanied by thousands of nonmilitary refugees﻿—which poured into Kanto after the collapse of the short-lived Far Eastern White Republic in Siberia. The greater part of these latest refugees eventually passed on to other parts of China, but some settled in Kanto and other districts of Manchuria, establishing small businesses, investing what remained of their savings, taking humble employment or living from hand to mouth. The lives of Russian refugees in China﻿—men and women frequently of a class not brought up to manual work﻿—are often terribly hard, and their stories heartrending. Suitable work is very difficult to find. Wages which will keep a Chinese or Korean workingman in what seems to him comfort, will not support the large frame or supply the more varied and aspiring wants of a Russian. The disorganization of life caused by the period of revolution, rootlessness, anarchy, bloodshed, and despair through which all these exiles have lived, has of course affected them psychologically﻿—especially the younger generation. Many Russian young men, therefore, at the time of this story, were following the line of least resistance by joining the Chinese army and taking part in Chinese civil wars﻿—often, poor souls, to lose their lives recklessly in causes that meant nothing to them.

===Chinese===

满洲一直有相当数目的俄国人。帝国主义时代的俄国久已觊觎这片富饶而顺手的国土；即使这些希望被俄国人败在日本人手里而推翻之后，俄国官方机构仍在满洲和关东的许多中心维持着。一九一七年的俄国革命，把帝制时代的一切官员同他们的权源切断了；这些人家因为没有别处可去，便同在中国的其他地方一样，继续住在满洲，靠积蓄尽力撑持度日。这些流亡者，又渐渐添上了已在西伯利亚的白俄中比较胆小或比较有远见的人。我假定我的马利宁家和奥斯塔片科家就属于这类早期的难民。这些零散的流亡者，不久便被一股白俄亡命者的大潮淹没——一支溃军的残部，带着成千的非军人难民，在西伯利亚短命的远东白俄共和国崩溃之后，涌进了关东。这批最晚的难民大部分后来转往中国其他地方，但也有一些在关东和满洲别的地区定居下来，开些小买卖，投下积蓄的所余，就卑微的差事，或者过一天算一天。在中国的俄国难民的生活——男女常常出身于从不惯于体力劳动的阶级——往往苦不堪言，他们的故事令人心碎。合适的工作极难找到。能叫一个中国或朝鲜工人觉得舒服的工钱，养不起一副俄国人的大骨架，也供不起俄国人更多样、更有志气的需求。革命时期造成的生活的解体——无根、无政府、流血、绝望，这些流亡者全从其中过来——自然在心理上影响了他们，尤其是年轻一代。因此，在这个故事发生的年代，许多俄国青年都顺着阻力最小的路走，投了中国的军队，参加中国的内战——可怜的人们，常常在一桩对他们毫无意义的战事里白白送了命。

===Original===

The part of Kanto in which my Malinins settled is very inaccessible, now that the U.S.S.R. have closed the Siberian border, though in actual distance not far from Vladivostok. The nearest railway is near the Korean border, several days’ walk south over trails often infested by brigands﻿—and even when the railway is reached, it does not﻿—or did not, at the time of this story﻿—run direct into southern Korea, or connect with the South Manchurian Railway. A traveler in possession of money and a passport would travel for two days by train across the northwestern corner of Korea to the Korean port of Seishin; whence he would take ship to another Korean port﻿—Gensan﻿—and from thence travel by train to Seoul. My Russian traveler would elect to walk all the way, partly because he could afford no better, and partly because in this way he could better escape the notice of the Japanese police, who, though present all along the border and in every considerable town of Kanto, concentrate particularly on the railways and harbors and are, perhaps naturally, very suspicious of Russians. It should be remembered that White Russians are everywhere on sufferance only. They have no political standing, no official representation, and no appeal in times of difficulty. Their Russian passports are symbols of a dead power, and the papers issued to them by Chinese local officials carry no great weight outside the neighborhoods where they are personally known.

===Chinese===

我的马利宁家所住的关东那一角，如今因为苏联封闭了西伯利亚边界，非常与世隔绝，虽然离符拉迪沃斯托克实际距离并不远。最近的铁路靠近朝鲜边界，往南要走几天的小路，路上常有土匪——就算到了铁路，它也不——至少在这个故事发生的年代不——直通南朝鲜，也不同南满铁路相接。一个有钱又有护照的旅客，可以坐两天火车，穿过朝鲜的西北角，到朝鲜港口清津；从那里坐船到另一个朝鲜港口——元山——再坐火车去汉城。我的俄国旅客宁可全程步行：一半因为更好的路他坐不起，一半因为这样更容易躲开日本警察的耳目——他们虽然遍布边界沿线和关东每个像样的镇子，尤其集中在铁路和港口，并且——也许理所当然——对俄国人疑心极重。应当记住，白俄在任何地方都只是勉强被容留。他们没有政治地位，没有官方代表，遇到困难也无处申诉。他们的俄国护照是一个死了的政权的符号；中国地方官吏发给他们的证件，在他们本人有名望的乡邻以外，也带不了多少分量。

===Original===

A point in my story which perhaps needs elucidation is the confusion of tongues between one Chinese and another. This will surprise no one who has lived in China. The spoken language differs so considerably from one province﻿—and even from one district﻿—to another, that it is not at all unusual for a European to be found acting as interpreter between, say, an English-speaking Chinese from Canton and a Mandarin-speaking Chinese settled in North China. Supposing the European to have lived a few years in Manchuria, he would almost certainly be able to make himself understood by the local Chinese population, whereas the Chinese newcomer from Canton might well be unable to make himself understood at all. Failing such an interpreter, however, and supposing the northerner and the Cantonese both to have some knowledge of the Chinese script (a certain degree of literacy is fairly widespread in China), they could communicate readily enough by means of writing, Chinese script being ideographic, not alphabetical, and universal throughout China.

Kanto is a small region, and perhaps I should therefore add that the picture of the village of Chi-tao-kou is a composite picture having no exact counterpart in fact, and any references to individuals or local affairs﻿—Russian, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, or missionary﻿—are purely imaginary.

S. B.

===Chinese===

我的故事里也许需要解释的一点，是中国人同中国人之间的言语不通。凡是住过中国的人，对这都不会惊讶。口语一省与一省——甚至一县与一县——相差太大，所以常常见到一个欧洲人当翻译，在一位置身华北、说官话的中国人，同一个来自广州、说英语的中国人之间传话。假定这个欧洲人在满洲住过几年，他几乎准能叫当地的中国人听懂，而那位刚从广州来的中国人倒很可能完全叫人听不懂。不过，倘若没有这样的翻译，再假定北方人和广州人都识得一些汉字（相当程度的识字在中国颇为普遍），他们大可以靠笔谈肠所欲言，因为汉字是表意的，不是拼音的，全中国通行。

关东是个小地方，所以我还该补一句：七道沟这幅村的图景，是一幅拼合的图，实际上并无一个完全相同的原物；凡涉及个人或地方事务之处——俄国人、中国人、日本人、朝鲜人、传教士——纯属虚构。

S. B.

## Author’s Request to Reader / 作者对读者的请求
===Original===

Please read the appendix of this book before beginning the story. It is intended that the parallel between the story of *The Faraway Bride* and the story given in the appendix be borne in mind throughout the reading of this book.

S. B.

===Chinese===

请先读本书的附录，再开始读正文。作者的本意是：读全书期间，请始终把《远方的新娘》的故事同附录所载的故事两相参照，记在心里。

斯·本。
